# NEOTH — Road to 1.0 GOLD (Leitlinien / Master Plan)

---

## 0. START HERE (fresh session)

**What is NEOTH?** A local-first sovereign personal-AI daemon written in Rust. Single operator, runs on the operator's machine. Core primitives: hemisphere council (Left/Right/Cerebellum provider routing), append-only WAL audit log (cryptographically signed frames), consent-gated tool execution, skills system, memory tiers (hot/warm/cold + groundtruth), channel adapters (Telegram, WhatsApp, Slack, Discord, Keet/Pears), and a coding-buddy subsystem. The binary is `neothd` (daemon + CLI). A GUI companion `neothd-gui` runs Slint. Architecture spec: `PLAN/00_DESIGN_v1.1_FINAL.md`.

**Current HEAD:** run `git log -1 --oneline` — intentionally not pinned here (a pinned hash goes stale; this line sat weeks wrong).

**What this file is:** The single source of truth for every task needed to reach the 1.0 GOLD release tag. Read it from top to bottom. Pick the first `[ ]` OPEN task in a workstream, do it, flip it to `[x]`, update `PLAN/PROGRESS_v1_0.md` in the same commit, push. Repeat.

**Rule: OPEN = `[ ]`, DONE = `[x]`. Nothing else.** No `[~]`, no "deferred", no "partial" markers. If something is half-shipped, the shipped part gets `[x]` and the remainder gets `[ ]`.

**How to pick work:** Go workstream by workstream (WS-A through WS-H). Within a workstream, prerequisites come first (tasks are ordered). Batch all work on a single file using the FILE-BATCH INDEX in section 4.

**Source artifacts:** All audit findings are in `REVIEWS/_gold_audit/`. The 6 agent audit files and `_wiring_results.md` are the primary input. Research files for Gold-TODO features are in `REVIEWS/_gold_audit/research/`.

## 1. Build & Test Recipe

Windows + MSVC only. `cargo` must run inside `vcvars64.bat` via the checked-in `SRC/_*.bat` wrappers (`_run.bat` no longer exists — recipe fixed 2026-07-02). Do not call raw `cargo` build/test/clippy from VSCode, PowerShell, Git Bash, or agent terminals on this checkout (metadata-only ops like `cargo tree`/`cargo update` are fine).

```bat
cmd.exe /d /s /c "SRC\_check.bat"   &rem cargo check -p neothd
cmd.exe /d /s /c "SRC\_clippy.bat"  &rem clippy -p neothd --lib --tests -- -D warnings
cmd.exe /d /s /c "SRC\_test.bat" <one-filter-token>   &rem cargo test -p neothd --lib %*
cmd.exe /d /s /c "SRC\_build.bat"   &rem full build; _feat.bat/_check_feat.bat for feature matrix
```

Each bat echoes `BUILD_EXIT=`/`TEST_EXIT=`/`CLIPPY_EXIT=` — read that token; never pipe a gate through `tail`.

Background it; read `RUN_EXIT=`; `tail` masks exit code — never pipe on gate runs.

- vcvars MUST be UNREDIRECTED — `>nul` breaks `cl.exe` → cc-rs `ToolNotFound` on ring/zstd-sys.
- `neothd-gui` is a BIN crate → `cargo test -p neothd-gui` (no `--lib`). os-clipboard feature: `--features os-clipboard`.
- Clippy gate: `clippy -p neothd --tests --no-deps -- -D warnings`
- CLI doc drift: adding/removing a CLI command/flag → `NEOTH_REGEN_CLI_DOCS=1 cargo test -p neothd --lib cli_commands_md_is_up_to_date`
- Per-item workflow: flip `[ ]`→`[x]` in `PROGRESS_v1_0.md` AND this file in the SAME commit; conventional commit; tests green before claiming done; `git push`.

## 2. WAL / Code Gotchas

- **0xA band FULL.** Free WAL slots: `0x5D-5F`, `0x69-6F`, `0x78-7F`, `0x85-8F`, `0x9D-9F`, `0xBE-BF`, `0xDD-DF`, `0xF6+`. (GR-078: 0x52-5C, 0xCE-CF and 0xDB-DC are ASSIGNED — removed from the free list; verified against wal/events.rs.)
- **Adding a WAL event = 5 sites:** const + compile-time band-assert + `EVENT_NAME_TABLE` + `all_event_codes_are_unique` + emit-site; plus `immediate_sync` default.
- **Exhaustive `Action` matches** live in `permissions/mod.rs` (`evaluate_strict/standard/elevated/full` + `lease_scope_for`) — adding a new `Action` variant requires updating all four arms.
- **Pastejack guards** must be `chars()`-based, not byte-slice based.
- **release.yml** uses default features — a default-OFF feature ships only if `--features X` added to the native step.
- **`#[test]` calling `wal::writer::spawn`** panics — use `#[tokio::test]`.
- **vcvars `>nul` breaks cl.exe** — arboard Wayland feat = `wl-clipboard-rs`; always unredirected.
- **Workflow `agent()` without `schema:`** eaten by fp-check Stop hook — use `schema:` or `SendMessage`.
- **`wal::scan` v2/zstd divergence in production:** `rollback.rs`, `indexer.rs`, `undo.rs`, `updater.rs` use bare `SEGMENT_HEADER_LEN` with no zstd decompress — silently skip compressed segments.
- **SQLite mutex over `.await`:** `ConnBorrow::Shared(shared.lock().await)` held for full LLM pipeline — `#[allow(clippy::await_holding_lock)]` is a suppression, not a fix.
- **Raw byte-slice panics:** `&s[..N]` where `s` has multibyte UTF-8 causes panic — use `chars().take(N).collect()` or `floor_char_boundary`.
- **0xDB, 0xDC, 0xDD** are claimed by multiple research plans — coordinate before assigning new WAL events in the 0xD* band.

**Verdict:** NEOTH has a strong security/crypto core (ed25519-signed WAL, consent gates, GDPR forget, PII redaction), but the surface over-promises on several fronts: migration (`apply` is a `bail!`), device-sync (memory ingest silently dropped), GUI (6 of 10 settings tabs are stubs), local TTS (deferred). Real exploitable bugs exist on operator paths: zip-slip in backup restore, path traversal in skill installer, LIKE wildcard mass-delete in GDPR forget, blocking code on async executor, and self-reported test results auto-promoting tasks. Both reviews are folded in: beta @ `35c94d2` (all A-01..A-10 still true); Roman @ `3ef9771` (14 net-new findings, CR-002 HLC is fixed, all others stand).

## WS-A — Security Hardening (GOLD-SEC-NN)

External/unauthenticated exploits first, then operator-data-loss, then defense-in-depth.

- [x] **GOLD-SEC-01** Add bearer-token check at the top of `route()` in neoth-relay before dispatching any POST/GET branch, or gate public-bind with mandatory `--token` flag — *files:* `SRC/neoth-relay/src/serve.rs`, `SRC/neoth-relay/src/main.rs` — *test:* unauthenticated request to public-bind returns 401 — *origin:* A-01 — ✅ **DONE:** `route()` auth-gate (constant-time compare, checked before dispatch incl. unknown paths) + `extract_bearer` + `public_bind_requires_token` predicate + fail-closed startup bail on non-loopback bind without `--token`/`NEOTH_RELAY_TOKEN`; token never logged. 49 tests / 0 fail, clippy `-D warnings` clean.
- [x] **GOLD-SEC-02** Before `entry.unpack(&dest)` in backup restore, canonicalize `dest` and bail if it does not start with `target_home` to prevent zip-slip path traversal — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/daemon/backup.rs` — *test:* tar with `../../../tmp/pwned` entry is rejected — *origin:* A-06 — ✅ **DONE:** added `safe_join` (rejects `..`/absolute/root components → always contained) used for every restore entry, PLUS reject symlink/hard-link entries outright (NEOTH backups are regular files+dirs only). Tests: `safe_join_allows_normal_paths_and_rejects_escapes` + `restore_rejects_symlink_entry` (tar-rs itself refuses to write a `..` entry — write-side defense in depth). 28 backup tests / 0 fail, clippy clean.
- [x] **GOLD-SEC-03** Call `crate::skills::creator::validate_skill_id(&manifest.id)?` before `target_skills_dir.join(&manifest.id)` in `install_from_local` — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/skills/installer.rs` — *test:* id of `"../etc/cron.d/x"` returns `Err` — *origin:* A-07 — ✅ **DONE:** replaced the weak empty-only check with `super::creator::validate_skill_id` (allows only `[a-zA-Z0-9_-]`, ≤64 → rejects `..`/`/`/`\`/empty) before the id becomes a path component. Tests: `install_from_local_rejects_path_traversal_id` + updated empty-id test. 13 installer tests / 0 fail, clippy clean.
- [x] **GOLD-SEC-04** Create `escape_like(s: &str) -> String` escaping `\`, `%`, `_` and add `ESCAPE '\\'` to every LIKE clause in `forget.rs` and `regions.rs` — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/memory/mod.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/memory/forget.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/memory/regions.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/memory/embeddings.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/cli/memory.rs` — *test:* `--forget "%"` does not delete entire memory tier — *origin:* A-08, A-44 — ✅ **DONE:** shared `memory::escape_like` (escapes `\`/`%`/`_`) + `... - [x] **GOLD-COR-01** Replace `std::process::exit(1)` in `cli/monitor.rs:266`, `cli/telemetry.rs:157`, `cli/wal.rs:229`, `cli/wal.rs:878`, `cli/doctor.rs:712` with `anyhow::bail!`; thread exit status through `Result` to main — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/cli/monitor.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/cli/telemetry.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/cli/wal.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/cli/doctor.rs` — *test:* `neoth monitor` exits cleanly after alert; WAL drain is not skipped — *origin:* A-03 — ✅ **DONE:** new `neothd::QuietExit(i32)` marker error (lib.rs) — all 5 deep `process::exit(1)` sites now `return Err(QuietExit(1).into())` so the stack unwinds (WAL flush-on-Drop + DB close + tokio runtime drain all run) and only the top `main` frame, after `worker.join()`, translates the marker into the requested exit code (no anyhow crash dump). Bonus: the verify-failure path is now testable — regression `verify_proof_bad_signature_returns_quiet_exit_not_process_exit` (sign key A, pin key B → `Err(QuietExit(1))`). wal 20 + monitor 1 + telemetry 5 + doctor 73 tests / 0 fail, clippy `--all-targets` clean.

- [x] **GOLD-COR-04** For security-bearing WAL frames (CHANNEL_PRIVILEGE_BLOCKED, HOOK_BLOCKED, INGEST_EXTRACTED, EMBED_PERSISTED, CONFIG_RELOADED): add `required_audit` flag that returns `Err` to the caller on append failure instead of logging-and-continuing — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/cli/serve.rs` — *test:* WAL writer failure for CHANNEL_PRIVILEGE_BLOCKED surfaces as error to pipeline — *origin:* A-11 — ✅ **DONE (operator-decided 2026-06-06):** new `emit_required_audit()` helper routes all 7 security-frame emit sites (1× CHANNEL_PRIVILEGE_BLOCKED, 3× HOOK_BLOCKED, INGEST_EXTRACTED, EMBED_PERSISTED, CONFIG_RELOADED). A write failure is no longer swallowed at `warn` — it logs at `error!` with a uniform greppable `audit_loss=true` + `event=<name>` field so `neoth monitor`'s rule engine can alert on a lost security record. **Deliberately NOT hard fail-closed** (Alex picked "loud non-silent" over the audit's literal `required_audit→Err`): at every one of these sites the guarded action — block/reject/drop, or persist/reload — has *already completed* by the time the frame is written, so aborting can't undo it (only incoherent post-side-effect state), and since `append` fails on a full WAL quota, propagating would turn a disk-cap into a DoS amplifier on exactly the security paths. Rationale captured in the helper doc-comment. Regression `emit_required_audit_survives_append_failure_without_aborting` (oversize → no panic, no propagate, only the valid frame lands) + existing `…writes_0x3c_frame` happy-path still green. 8 serve tests / 0 fail, clippy clean.

The lock is on the `.pid` file itself, but **readers stay un-blocked** (unix flock is advisory; windows shares read) so `live_daemon_pid` (used by `neoth ingest`) still works. `PidGuard` now holds the locked `File` (released on drop/crash → no stale-lock problem; PID-recycling false-positives gone too). Verified on this Windows box: 9 pidfile tests incl. `acquire_rejects_when_another_holds_the_lock`, `concurrent_acquire_only_one_holds_the_lock` (8-thread race → exactly 1 winner = the A-16 regression), `live_daemon_pid_reads_through_held_lock`, `lock_releases_on_drop_so_next_acquire_succeeds`. daemon:: 413 / 0 fail, clippy `--tests` clean. - [x] **GOLD-COR-19** Parallelize `coding/dispatcher.rs` serial session dispatcher — replace serial-for-now comment with concurrent hemisphere dispatch — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/coding/dispatcher.rs` — *test:* two hemisphere workers run concurrently — *origin:* CR-031 — ✅ **DONE (2-lens design gremium + rust-reviewer APPROVE):** `dispatch_session_with_apply` was a serial loop (pick one Backlog task → `worker.execute().await` → process). New loop: `pick_batch` (one Backlog task per BOUND hemisphere in a single pass; unbound-hemisphere tasks Blocked inline → `tasks_unassigned`; `claimed` HashSet guarantees one task/hemisphere so no worker runs concurrently with itself) → trim to remaining `max_tasks` → transition all InProgress + emit 0x77 (serial DB) → run the batch's `worker.execute()` CONCURRENTLY via `futures_util::future::join_all` on THIS task (no `tokio::spawn` → no Send/'static bound; conn stays put) → process each result SERIALLY (the original match arms verbatim; retry/spiral/repetition/greeting-regression/apply state all mutated one-at-a-time). Retries re-queue to Backlog → picked in a later batch. Budget preserved (trim before increment; the all-Left budget test still gets exactly 2). Race-freedom is borrow-checker-enforced (no `unsafe`/interior-mutability; conn + state provably absent from the concurrent futures — confirmed by rust-reviewer across all 8 attack vectors). - [x] **GOLD-COR-31** Verify `clear_kv_cache()` placement in `ouro/forward.rs` and `ouro/quantized_forward.rs`; move to once-per-`forward` call if correct — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/providers/ouro/forward.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/providers/ouro/quantized_forward.rs` — *test:* real-weight inference test (mark `#[ignore]` + document in test) — *origin:* C-23 — ✅ **DONE (verified + real bug fixed):** A 3-lens research gremium (code-semantics + reference-architecture + fix-options) + the source-paper **arXiv:2510.25741 "Scaling Latent Reasoning via Looped Language Models"** settled this. **The per-loop `clear_kv_cache` is CORRECT and must stay** — the paper confirms prefill needs a *fresh* KV cache per recurrent step (reuse degrades >10 GSM8K points), and removing it would make loop iteration N re-concat the same positions (corrupt double-attention). So the task's suggested "move to once-per-forward" is **wrong** and was NOT done. The real defect is in the **adapter decode loop**: it did true incremental decoding (1 token + growing `seqlen_offset`), but the per-loop clear wipes the cross-call cache at `loop_idx=0` of every decode step, so each new token attended only to ITSELF — silently context-blind, incoherent generation (no crash; mask is `None` for `seq_len==1`). Fixed via **full-resequence decode** (`adapter.rs`: new pure `decode_context_ids` feeds prompt + all generated tokens at `seqlen_offset=0` each step; `forward()` narrows to the last token internally → correct logits over full context). Zero model-file changes, routes through the already-tested full-sequence forward path. The paper's perf-optimal **two-tier "last-step-reuse" persistent cache is the follow-up GOLD-COR-36** (needs real-weight validation, deferred per verify-before-done). Tests: `decode_context_ids_feeds_full_running_sequence_not_single_token` (runnable, proves full-context decode) + `local_ouro_decode_is_context_aware_not_degenerate_repetition` (`#[ignore]` real-weight regression via the existing `NEOTH_OURO_TEST_REPO_PATH` harness). providers::ouro 85/0 (8 ignored), clippy `-D warnings` clean.

- [x] **GOLD-COR-33** Acquire DB lock only for synchronous DB ops in `serve.rs`; release before any `.await` in `ConnBorrow::Shared` pipeline path (CR-010) — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/cli/serve.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/profile/runner.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/profile/mod.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/cli/chat.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/cli/profile.rs` — *test:* concurrent channel requests do not serialize on DB lock — *origin:* CR-010, A-15 — ✅ **DONE (scope corrected by research):** the shared `views.db` tokio-Mutex was held across the WHOLE `profile::run_pipeline` (incl. its seconds-long LLM extract) in the serve.rs `ConnBorrow::Shared` path, serializing every channel's post-reply profile pipeline on the DB lock. Research established `run_pipeline`'s LLM stage (`extract_delta`) does NOT touch `conn` (works on the in-memory attributed window), so the lock can be scoped to the brief sync DB stages. A naive per-call Owned connection was rejected: `run_pipeline` runs `replay_once` (WAL→views) first and concurrent connections would race the replay offset — the shared connection serializes replay+writes. Fix: `run_pipeline` now takes a `PipelineConn` enum (`Owned(&mut Connection)` for chat/CLI/tests — a zero-cost reborrow; `Shared(&Arc<Mutex<Connection>>)` for the daemon) and `lock()`s the connection only around stages 1 (window read), 5 (redactions read), 5b (gated approval), 6 (apply write) — releasing it around the LLM extract. serve.rs takes a short lock for `replay_once` then passes `Shared`; the `ConnBorrow` enum is gone. The 14 Owned call sites (chat.rs, profile.rs, 12 runner tests) changed to `PipelineConn::Owned`; `run_pipeline`'s future stays `!Send` (rusqlite conn across awaits) so the daemon still drives it under `block_in_place`. Tests: all 17 existing run_pipeline tests pass unchanged (logic preserved) + new `cor33_shared_conn_lock_released_during_llm_extract` (a `BlockingLlmMock` blocks mid-extract; the test proves `shared.lock()` is acquirable while the LLM is in flight — red-before/green-after via `join!`, run_pipeline's future being `!Send` rules out `tokio::spawn`). profile::runner 18/0, cli::serve 8/0, cli::profile 36/0, clippy `-D warnings` clean.
- [x] **GOLD-COR-34** Wrap all 11 remaining detached `tokio::spawn` in `cli/serve.rs` in JoinSet for graceful shutdown and WAL drain — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/cli/serve.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/channels/webhook_listener.rs` — *test:* `SIGTERM` allows WAL drain before exit — *origin:* CR-011 — ✅ **DONE (task premise corrected by a 2-lens research gremium):** there are **NO** truly-detached `tokio::spawn` in serve.rs — all 11 direct spawns are bound to named handles and already `.abort()`+`.await`-ed in the shutdown block. The ONE real fire-and-forget WAL-writing spawn is the **COR-08 `DISPATCH_GATE` Meta fan-out in `webhook_listener.rs:415`** (its own comment deferred the drain to COR-34). Those dispatch tasks hold a `WalWriterHandle` clone via their `cfg` Arc, so `writer_join.await` currently blocks on them *accidentally* — and unboundedly (a hung provider turn hangs daemon exit). - [x] **GOLD-COR-36** Ouro perf: implement the paper's two-tier "last-step-reuse" KV cache (arXiv:2510.25741) so decode is O(n) again instead of the COR-31 full-resequence O(n²) — add a cross-call persistent `cross_cache` (final-loop K/V per past token) alongside the per-loop intra-call scratch; promote the new token's final-loop K/V after the last recurrent iteration; thread an `is_last_loop` signal into `OuroAttention::forward`; also fix the latent `seq_len>1 && seqlen_offset>0` causal-mask shape mismatch surfaced by the COR-31 gremium — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/providers/ouro/layers.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/providers/ouro/quantized_layers.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/providers/ouro/forward.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/providers/ouro/quantized_forward.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/providers/ouro/adapter.rs` — *test:* real-weight context-coherence parity vs the COR-31 full-resequence baseline + a synthetic non-zero-weight context-sensitivity test — *origin:* COR-31 follow-up (perf), arXiv:2510.25741 — ✅ **DONE (3 commits `e9fd746`+`f39f578`+this).** **Verify-first + a 4-lens schema'd gremium (`w1lhceog3`) resolved a self-CONTRADICTORY task premise:** it demanded BOTH "identical logits vs the COR-31 baseline" AND the paper's "last-step-reuse O(n)" — mutually exclusive. Last-step-reuse caches ONLY the final loop, so non-final loops attend over stale final-loop K/V → an APPROXIMATION, not identical. **The identical+O(n) design is a PER-LOOP cache** (NOT the task's single-cross_cache+is_last_loop): each attention holds `kv_caches: Vec<Option<(K,V)>>` of length `total_ut_steps`, indexed by `loop_idx`. Past tokens (0..t-1) at loop L are causally independent of token t, so a past token's loop-L K/V is deterministically identical across decode steps → caching per-loop lets incremental decode (1 new token at a growing `seqlen_offset`) compute BIT-IDENTICAL attention inputs to full-resequence in O(n) forward passes (vs O(n²)). `forward_loops` drops the inner per-loop clear; resets all slots only at `seqlen_offset==0` (so full-resequence@0 is unchanged — the mask "fix" was a non-issue: the existing `causal_attention_mask` already handles offset>0). Native + quantized both migrated (else Q8 silently stays O(n²)). Adapter `run_ouro_forward` gains the O(n) single-token decode gated behind `NEOTH_OURO_KV_CACHE_MODE=per_loop`, **default = the proven COR-31 full_resequence** until a real-weight run certifies the candle tensor-cat path (promotion to a `freedom.yaml::ouro.kv_cache_mode` default-flip is the follow-up). **THE ORACLE (the key insight): the existing synthetic harness uses ZERO weights → a parity test on it is VACUOUS** (degenerate context-independent output catches no mask/RoPE bug). Built NON-ZERO deterministic weights so the model is genuinely context-sensitive (asserted), then `per_loop_cache_decode_matches_full_resequence_baseline` (native + quantized) proves `forward([1,2,3,4]@0)` == `[forward([1,2,3]@0); forward([4]@3)]` within 1e-4. Plus the `#[ignore]` `local_ouro_per_loop_decode_matches_full_resequence_on_real_weights` cert via `NEOTH_OURO_TEST_REPO_PATH`. providers::ouro 85/0 (9 ignored), full lib suite 7033 passed (only the `C:\Temp` env-quirk red), clippy `-D warnings` clean. **WS-C COMPLETE (36/36).**

## WS-D — Feature Wiring: Complete Built-but-Unwired Features (GOLD-WIRE-NN)

Operator hard rule: unwired modules are FEATURES TO WIRE, never deletions.

- [x] **GOLD-WIRE-01** Wire `coding/tool_router` into `ProviderWorker::execute()`: call `model_profile::get_profile` + `tool_router::routing_mode_for_profile` at top; dispatch TwoStage selector call before task prompt on small-context models — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/coding/provider_worker.rs` — *test:* TwoStage mode fires a second provider.complete call; Direct mode skips it; unknown category reply falls back to Direct — *origin:* _wiring_results.md tool_router
  - ✅ DONE: `ProviderWorker` gained a `model_name` field (threaded from `cli/code.rs::build_worker_set` via `cfg.inference.slot_for(role).model`, `""` when unset → unknown-default 32 k → Direct, a safe no-extra-call fallback). `execute()` now computes `get_profile(&model_name, 0)` → `routing_mode_for_profile`: **TwoStage** (≤ 16 384 ctx, e.g. local deepseek-coder/qwen) fires a Stage-1 `select_tool_category()` selector turn (`build_selector_prompt()` → `provider.complete`), parses the reply leniently (`parse_category_reply`: trim+lowercase, exact `from_str` then first-word fallback), and primes the Stage-2 task prompt with that category's description + `category_member_hint` vocabulary; **Direct** (> 16 384) skips the selector entirely. A failed selector call OR an unparseable category reply yields `None` → the task prompt is built plain (the Direct shape), so the task still completes. **Note (verify-first):** `ProviderWorker` is free-form (no tool-schema injection / no tool registry yet — the module doc flags that), so Stage-2 priming is a lightweight focus hint rather than schema-pruning; this is the wiring point the `tool_router` doc itself names ("the actual Stage-1→Stage-2 handshake belongs in `ProviderWorker::execute()`"). `build_task_prompt` gained a `tool_hint: Option<ToolCategory>` param. Tests (counting mock provider): `two_stage_model_fires_selector_then_task` (2 calls), `direct_model_skips_selector` (1 call), `unknown_category_reply_falls_back_to_direct_task` (2 calls, no hint, task still done), `build_task_prompt_injects_category_hint_when_present`, `parse_category_reply_handles_clean_firstword_and_unknown`. coding::provider_worker 16/0, clippy `-D warnings` clean.
- [x] **GOLD-WIRE-02** Wire `recall/conversational` into `chat.rs::run_chat_with`: after RAW_TEXT WAL write, add `detect_recall_intent` check; on match, query memory and format reply without LLM call — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/cli/chat.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/cli/serve.rs` — *test:* "Weißt du noch als wir über X geredet haben?" triggers memory reply; no PROVIDER_REQUEST WAL frame written — *origin:* _wiring_results.md conversational_recall
  - ✅ DONE (CLI path; channel path split to WIRE-02b after adversarial review — see below). Built with an ultracode workflow pipeline: **understand/blueprint** (4 parallel readers → synthesis) → build → **4-lens adversarial review** → fix. New shared `pub(crate) async fn cli::recall::answer_conversational_recall(prompt, db_path) -> Option<String>` (hot-tier `recall_fts`→`recall_like`, best-effort: any DB error → "nothing found", never an `Err`; sync rusqlite wrapped in `spawn_blocking` off the async worker). `run_chat_with` calls it AFTER the RAW_TEXT write + BEFORE the PROVIDER_REQUEST block: on a recall match it `println!`s the formatted reply, runs the standard `drop(writer)+writer_join.await` teardown, and `return Ok(())` — so **no PROVIDER_REQUEST/RESPONSE frame is written**. Integration test `recall_intent_short_circuits_without_provider_or_request_frame` (NeverCalledProvider asserts `complete()` never fires + the only WAL frame after the header is the verbatim RAW_TEXT) + 2 hermetic helper unit tests (seeded idx_episode hit / empty-DB miss). **The review caught a CRITICAL latent flaw the wiring EXPOSED: `detect_recall_intent` (now load-bearing) hijacked normal prompts** — "weißt du was, lass uns X bauen", "was haben wir hier für einen Bug?", "wann habe ich das nächste Meeting?", "remember that the build is broken, fix it", "do you remember the API for X? write me code" all silently dropped the LLM turn. **Hardened the detector** (`recall/conversational.rs`): removed the idiomatic-filler / diagnostic openers (`weißt du was`/`weisst du was`, `was haben wir`, `wann habe ich`, `remember that`) + added a `topic_is_compound` guard (a topic with mid-string `?`/`!` after clean-up is a rhetorical-lead-in compound → fall through to the LLM). New regression test `no_intent_on_idiomatic_filler_and_compound_prompts`; all existing positive detect tests still green. recall::conversational 22/0, cli::recall 11/0, cli::chat 47/0, clippy `-D warnings` clean.
- [x] **GOLD-WIRE-02b** Wire conversational-recall into the CHANNEL path (`serve_pipeline.rs build_pipeline_handler`) — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/cli/serve_pipeline.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/cli/recall.rs` — *test:* channel recall reply is operator-scoped + passes the ChannelSend gate + no provider call — *origin:* GOLD-WIRE-02 review split — ✅ **DONE** (2-lens adversarial review: security + behavior-preservation, both APPROVE). **Verify-first reframed the scope model:** the review's "scope the query by operator_id/channel" can't work — the searchable conversational text lives in **RAW_TEXT-derived `idx_episode` rows whose `channel/sender_id/operator_id` are NULL** (`memory/indexer.rs:229`); the CHANNEL_INGRESS rows carry hashed sender but their text is a `"[INGRESS] N bytes"` placeholder. A `WHERE channel=? AND sender_id=?` scope returns ZERO rows; no-scope returns EVERY operator's text. The text-bearing rows are fundamentally un-attributable to a sender. **So the boundary is enforced at the RECIPIENT, not in SQL:** new pure `recall::channel_recall_authorized(sender_uuid, operator_uuid)` serves channel recall ONLY to the **provable operator** (`inbound.human_uuid == config.channel_weights.operator_human_uuid`, both `Some`) — deliberately stricter than `channel_weights::learn_factor` (an unpinned operator gets NO channel recall, vs learn's `(_,None)=>true`). `human_uuid` is resolved **server-side** from the platform-verified `(channel, sender_id, chat_id)` triple (`channels/identity.rs`) — NOT attacker-supplied — so a sender can't spoof the operator's uuid without authenticating as them on-platform. A non-operator (or unpinned-operator) recall-shaped prompt falls through to the normal LLM turn (no memory read out). **Egress gate (b):** extracted the inline egress tail into a shared `release_channel_reply()` helper (PreEgress hooks → ChannelSend gate, lease-aware, FailClosed → CHANNEL_EGRESS) used by BOTH the recall short-circuit AND the normal model reply — so a no-provider reply is gated **identically** (no policy drift); a gate-Deny / PreEgress-Block returns `Ok(None)` and writes NO CHANNEL_EGRESS (no false attestation). The recall reply attests honest provenance (`provider="local-recall"`, `model="conversational-recall"`, no tokens). Behavior-preservation review confirmed the normal-path refactor is byte-identical (sender_hash, recipient, egress JSON shape all unchanged). *tests:* `channel_recall_authorized_only_for_provable_operator` (5 cases), `release_channel_reply_allows_at_standard_and_emits_recall_egress` (Allow→Some + 1 egress frame attesting local-recall), `release_channel_reply_denies_at_strict_and_writes_no_egress` (Strict FailClosed→None, 0 egress frames). serve_pipeline 10/0, cli::recall 12/0, `cargo check` + clippy `-D warnings` clean.

- [x] **GOLD-WIRE-05** Wire `claude_pid_hunter::scan_stuck_processes` into `cli/doctor.rs` as a new check entry; add doctor check that detects deadlocked claude processes — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/cli/doctor.rs`, `SRC/neothd/src/providers/claude_pid_hunter.rs` — *test:* stuck claude process surfaces as WARN in doctor output — *origin:* _wiring_results.md claude_cli_recovery — ✅ **DONE.** New `neoth doctor` check `check_stuck_claude_processes` (registered in `run_all_checks` as #30) runs the new sync `claude_pid_hunter::scan_stuck_processes_blocking` (the doctor registry is sync; the existing async `scan_stuck_processes` keeps its `spawn_blocking` for daemon callers) and renders via a pure `stuck_processes_outcome(&[StuckProcess]) -> CheckOutcome` (empty→PASS, any→WARN listing pid + idle-minutes; never FAIL — a hung process is recoverable). Added a CHECK_DOCS runbook entry (`--explain`/`--list-checks`) + bumped both 29→30 count-pins. **The adversarial 2-lens review (correctness + honesty) caught a real latent bug the wiring EXPOSED:** `scan_blocking` did two `sysinfo` refreshes back-to-back with no delay, but sysinfo 0.32 needs `MINIMUM_CPU_UPDATE_INTERVAL` (200ms) between them or `cpu_usage()` reads ~0 for EVERY process → every claude past the 15-min floor would be false-flagged stuck. Fixed with `thread::sleep(MINIMUM_CPU_UPDATE_INTERVAL)` between the refreshes (off-reactor via `spawn_blocking` on the daemon path; only the idle doctor-CLI thread on the sync path). **Honesty:** the doctor output references only real recovery (`kill`/`taskkill`), never the module's `stuck_hint()` which named the unbuilt `neoth doctor stuck-clean` / `neoth chat reset --force` — and (review NIT) I rewrote `stuck_hint()` to drop those phantom commands (now points at the real `neoth doctor` WARN + manual kill), flipped its test from PINNING the phantoms to GUARDING against them, and de-lied the module + `StuckProcess` struct docs. *tests:* `stuck_processes_outcome_warns_and_lists_pid` (WARN+pid, asserts no phantom cmds), `_passes_when_none`, `check_stuck_claude_processes_skips_when_not_claude_cli`, `stuck_hint_points_at_real_recovery_only`. cli::doctor 76/0, claude_pid_hunter 13/0, clippy `-D warnings` clean.

The single `send_and_wait_with_timeouts` in `complete_tmux_uncached` is now inside a classify-driven `loop`: a pure `plan_tmux_retry(signal, attempt) -> TmuxRetryPlan` (Retry{step} | Surface{class,hint}) classifies each failure and the loop honours it — **Auth surfaces immediately** (`max_attempts=0`, never retries), **EmptyStdout / a vanished pane retry once** (the latter killing + rotating the session under a fresh `rotation_seq` name), **Transient retries with exponential backoff** — every class bounded by `max_attempts` (≤4 sends total). Outcome→`FailureSignal` mapping: empty `Ok` / `HardTimeoutNoOutput` → `exit_code=Some(0)` (EmptyStdout); `PaneDisappeared` → its Display carries the SessionCollision needle; `Tmux(e)` → `e.to_string()` (Auth-keyword-or-transient). - [x] **GOLD-WIRE-07b** Daemon HNSW snapshot auto-freshness — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/memory/snapshot_refresh.rs` (new), `SRC/neothd/src/cli/serve.rs` — *test:* gated/stale decision is correct; below-ceiling + absent-db + non-hnsw are no-ops — *origin:* GOLD-WIRE-07 split — ✅ **DONE** (3-lens design panel + a verify-first correction of the panel's own verdict). **The panel's "in-memory `Arc<RwLock<EmbeddingIndex>>` warm index + add-on-upsert" verdict was REFUTED by grepping the writers:** `idx_embedding` is written by the daemon (channel media, `serve_pipeline.rs`) AND the **separate `neoth ingest` CLI process** (`cli/ingest.rs`, the bulk writer). A daemon in-memory index would `add` only its own channel-media vectors, MISS every CLI-ingested vector, and — if saved — CLOBBER a good `neoth memory --rebuild-index` snapshot with an incomplete one. **Correct design (reads SQLite = the source of truth shared across both processes):** a daemon periodic task (`tokio::time::interval`, 30 min, first tick fires at boot) that REBUILDS the on-disk snapshot from SQLite via the existing `embeddings::rebuild_index` (tmp+atomic-rename, crash-safe + concurrent-cold-load-safe) when it has gone stale — so the cross-process CLI cold-load stays fresh without operator action. New `memory/snapshot_refresh.rs`: pure `should_refresh_snapshot(backend_is_hnsw, corpus, snap_mtime, newest_embedding)` (gated on backend=hnsw + corpus past the brute-force ceiling + snapshot absent-with-vectors OR stale, reusing the doctor's mtime-vs-`MAX(created_at)` staleness probe) + `refresh_snapshot_once(home, backend_is_hnsw)` (best-effort, blocking → `spawn_blocking`). Wired into `run_serve` as `snapshot_refresh_handle` (gated `Option<JoinHandle>`, registered in `worker_watch`, aborted in shutdown — writes NO WAL frames so order-independent vs the writer drain). **DEFERRED (genuinely separable): the in-memory warm index + daemon-IPC hot-serve** (lets CLI recall skip the cold-load by querying the daemon — a new listener + protocol), per-kind sharding, and tunable `M`/`ef`/`ef_search`. This slice delivers the actual gap (snapshot freshness) at low risk. *tests:* `should_refresh_snapshot` 6 branch cases (not-hnsw / below-ceiling / absent-with-vectors / stale / fresh / no-vectors) + `refresh_snapshot_once` 3 no-op gates (non-hnsw / absent-db / below-ceiling-no-snapshot-written). memory::snapshot_refresh 9/0, `cargo check` + clippy `-D warnings` clean.

- [x] **GOLD-ARCH-03** Extract `wal::scan::for_each_frame(seg_bytes, cb)` handling v1/v2+zstd uniformly; migrate all 57+ callers (incl. `rollback.rs`, `indexer.rs`, `undo.rs`, `updater.rs`) — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/wal/` (new module), 57+ callers — *test:* v2/zstd segment frames are not silently skipped in indexer and rollback — *origin:* C-09, D-01, A-65 — ✅ **DONE** (3 commits: part 1 foundation+indexer, part 2 rollback+undo, part 3 the rest). New `wal::scan::for_each_frame(seg_bytes, cb)` decodes v1 (60 B header) AND v2/zstd (61 B header + decompressed blob) uniformly via `compaction::logical_segment_bytes`, so a compressed segment's frames are read instead of silently yielding ZERO. **Verify-first corrected the "57+ callers" gross overcount** — the real production frame-scanners are ~16; the rest of the `SEGMENT_HEADER_LEN` hits are test fixtures, the constant def, writer header-writes, or already-v2-correct (`cli/verify.rs`, and `cli/wal.rs::{show,collect_proof}` which already do `parse_segment_header`+`decompress_frames`). Migrated read-only scanners to `for_each_frame`: `memory/indexer.rs::replay_once` (kept its resume-cursor walk on logical bytes), `cli/rollback.rs::{find_snapshot_at,collect_snapshots}`, `cli/undo.rs`, `cli/updater.rs::load_results_from_wal`, `cli/checkpoint.rs::find_latest_snapshot`, `cli/kanban.rs`, `permissions/audit.rs::audit_segment`, `cron/runner.rs::aggregate_profile_snapshot`, `daemon/monitor_cron.rs::{count_crc_frames_in_segment,latest_channel_activity_in_segment}`; and `cli/wal.rs::collect_stats` + `cli/hooks.rs::collect_hook_trace` adopted the in-file `parse_segment_header`+`decompress_frames` idiom (they need a torn-tail `bad_frames` count / a `limit` early-stop that `for_each_frame` abstracts away). **`wal/recovery.rs::scan_tail` is the SPECIAL case — deliberately NOT migrated to `for_each_frame`:** its returned offset drives a physical `set_len` TRUNCATION, so it must walk RAW frames + return RAW-FILE offsets. Verify-first established the seal model: production WAL is always v1 (`wal::spawn`→`CompressionPolicy::None`; Zstd3 is test-only), and even under Zstd3 the LIVE segment scan_tail resumes has a RAW body (compression happens only at clean finalize, after which the segment is rotated away, never resumed) — so decompressing it (the flag is set but the body is raw) would ERROR and is wrong. The real latent bug there was the hardcoded 60-byte start offset misaligning a 61-byte v2 header → fixed by deriving `header_len` from `parse_segment_header`. **`wal/redact.rs::scan_and_redact` (privacy guarantee for `memory::forget`):** the hardcoded 60 offset would magic-mismatch on a live-v2 segment, break the loop, and SILENTLY scrub nothing while the caller reports success — a privacy hole; fixed by deriving `header_len` AND refusing (loud error) a *sealed* compressed segment (distinguished from a live-v2 raw body by peeking for a frame MAGIC at `header_len`). The decompress→redact→recompress rewrite for sealed segments is the genuine multi-day deferral → `GOLD-ARCH-03b` (`// TODO` in place). `cluster/wal_sync.rs::collect_gossipable_frames` audited + documented as correct-by-caller (the caller derives the right `header_len` and skips compressed segments; gossip needs raw wire bytes + a cursor, so `for_each_frame` is intentionally NOT used). `cli/migrate.rs` left as-is (v1→v2 tool, guarded `== V1` so the 60-byte slice is sound by construction). **Tests:** `wal::scan` 5 (incl. `compressed_v2_yields_all_frames` — the keystone); regression tests proving v2 frames are seen / privacy holds: indexer `replay_indexes_frames_from_a_v2_compressed_segment`, rollback+undo v2-segment tests, recovery `scan_v2_live_segment_*` (2), redact `scan_redacts_matching_frames_in_a_v2_live_segment` + `scan_refuses_to_redact_a_sealed_compressed_segment`, wal `stats_counts_frames_in_a_v2_compressed_segment`, hooks `collect_hook_trace_reads_frames_from_a_v2_compressed_segment`. Full lib suite **7110 passed**, the only 2 reds are pre-existing + unrelated (WIRE-11's stale `cli-commands.md` + the `C:\Temp\Cargo.toml` env quirk); clippy `-p neothd --tests --no-deps -D warnings` clean.
- [x] **GOLD-ARCH-03b** Redact sealed compressed (v2/zstd) WAL segments: extend `wal::redact::scan_and_redact` with a decompress → redact-in-logical-space → recompress → atomic-`.tmp`-rename path so `memory::forget` can scrub PII from a finalised compressed segment — *files:* `SRC/neothd/src/wal/redact.rs` — *test:* a topic in a sealed v2 segment is zeroed + the segment re-seals + re-decompresses clean — *origin:* GOLD-ARCH-03 split — ✅ **DONE** (2026-06-13): `scan_and_redact` now ROUTES a sealed compressed segment (zstd blob body, distinguished from a live-v2 raw body by peeking for a frame MAGIC at `header_len`) to the new `redact_sealed_compressed_segment`: reads the file, preserves the 61-byte v2 header **byte-for-byte**, `decompress_frames` the blob (with the existing 256 MiB zip-bomb cap), redacts matching frames in logical space via the new `redact_frames_in_buffer` (same zero-payload + `EventFlags::REDACTED` + CRC-recompute as `redact_frame_in_place`; same loud tamper-refusal on a bad MAGIC mid-buffer), `compress_frames` the result, and atomically rewrites via `.wal.redact.tmp` → fsync → rename (handle dropped first — Windows won't rename over an open file). **No-match ⇒ file left byte-identical** (no needless recompress). **Offset-space correctness (load-bearing):** `frames_redacted` are recorded as LOGICAL offsets (`header_len + pos`) — the same coordinate space `cli/verify.rs` walks via `compaction::logical_segment_bytes` — so an operator-signed `0xF3 REDACTION_MARKER` reclassifies the resulting compaction-marker HMAC mismatch to PASS instead of a bogus FAIL. **Tests (6 new):** redact-a-match-in-sealed (re-seals + re-decompresses clean + chain-of-evidence anchors preserved), idempotent re-run, no-match-byte-identical, corrupt-body-MAGIC-bails, truncated-frame-in-body-bails, and the e2e `run_verify_reclassifies_a_redaction_inside_a_compressed_segment` (redact frame inside compressed seg → FAIL with no 0xF3 → operator 0xF3 → PASS — proves the logical-offset alignment). The old `scan_refuses_to_redact_a_sealed_compressed_segment` test was replaced (behaviour flipped from refuse → redact). **Hardened per a 4-lens adversarial review (data-loss / integrity-offset / security-DoS / correctness):** the logical walk now BAILS (not silent partial-walk) on a truncated/oversized `total_len` or trailing-undecodable bytes (a sealed body is byte-complete by construction, so any shortfall is tamper — closes the HIGH truncated-tail privacy hole all-but-one lens flagged); a length-consistency check (`reserved_len`+`payload_len`+CRC ≤ `total_len`) refuses a crafted header before it can panic a slice; the atomic rewrite uses a per-invocation unique tmp (pid+counter, no concurrent-clobber), removes the tmp on rename failure, and fsyncs the parent dir on Unix (GDPR durability — the rename survives a crash); flags-byte offset hoisted to a single `FLAGS_OFFSET_IN_HEADER_BODY` const shared by both redaction paths. Full lib **7714 passed / 0 failed**; clippy clean; `wal/redact.rs`-only (+ a verify.rs test) — disjoint of all parallel-hot files.

Both compile sites — the `fires` matcher check AND the `Replace` action — now call it, killing the per-dispatch double-compile + recompile. There is NO `ClusterId` newtype (the plan mis-named it; the "cluster_key" is a different namespace the gossip_wire newtype deliberately guards against). Merging a UUID-session-id with a pubkey would DESTROY the intentional swap-prevention type-safety. **CORRECTED SCOPE:** (1) rename `mod.rs::PeerId` (UUID) → `PeerSessionId` to kill the dangerous same-name-different-meaning collision (two `PeerId` types in different modules mean different things); (2) merge the TWO genuinely-redundant pubkey newtypes (`gossip_wire::PeerId`-as-pubkey + `pears_election::PeerPubkey`) into ONE canonical pubkey type. NOT a 3→1 merge. Both legs are broad multi-file renames across `cluster/*` (every `PeerId` use must be disambiguated UUID-vs-pubkey first) → do with fresh context + a compiler-driven rename; verify the swap-prevention invariant survives. — ✅ **DONE (2026-06-15, the corrected 2-leg scope, compiler-driven).** **Leg 1:** `mod.rs::PeerId` (UUID-session) → **`PeerSessionId`** — killed the same-name collision with the pubkey-shaped `gossip_wire::PeerId` (the real hazard). **Leg 2:** merged the TWO redundant pubkey newtypes (`gossip_wire::PeerId` + `pears_election::PeerPubkey`) into ONE canonical **`PeerPubkey`** defined in `cluster/mod.rs` beside `PeerSessionId` — superset shape (Clone/Debug/Default/Serialize/Deserialize/PartialEq/Eq/PartialOrd/Ord/Hash + `#[serde(transparent)]` + `new`/`as_str`). **Swap-prevention invariant SURVIVES** (the whole point): `PeerSessionId`(UUID) and `PeerPubkey`(hex) are DISTINCT newtypes → the compiler rejects assigning one where the other is expected; NOT a 3→1 merge. **Wire byte-identical:** the canonical keeps `#[serde(transparent)]`, so the gossip `VectorClock` (BTreeMap key) + `GossipFrame.origin` serialise as bare strings exactly as pre-ARCH-21 (`gossip_wire::tests::peer_id_transparent_serde_form` now covers the canonical). 9 files: `cluster/{mod,gossip_wire,wal_sync,heartbeat,pears_election,pears_routing,pears_peer_discovery}.rs` + `cli/cluster.rs` (the lone out-of-cluster UUID consumer, via `RoutingDecision::Remote`). **Pre-existing/behavior-preserved/NOT touched:** `wal_sync` seeds the gossip self-id `PeerPubkey::new(uuid::now_v7())` — a UUID in the pubkey-typed VC key; predates ARCH-21, the rename keeps it byte-for-byte. New `mod.rs` test `peer_identity_types_are_distinct_and_pubkey_is_wire_transparent` pins distinctness (type-level helpers) + the transparent wire shape. cluster:: **337/0** (whole lib compiled), clippy `-D warnings` clean. `cluster/*`-only — disjoint of the parallel CHANNELS lane.

- [x] **Alex-02** ✅ DONE — OpenClaw/claudeclaw already deep-read (GOLD-ADOPT-07); the one real reliability steal is GOLD-ADAPT-CLAW-01 below. NEOTH ahead on the rest. See WS-I "Deep-read derived adaptations" below (this file). - READ THE OPENCLAW REPO - copy locally, deploy agents, agents should investigate features, logic, and everything we should adopt from OPENCLAW to perfect NEOTH (ui elements, logic, algorithms, functions, features etc.) - EXPAND THIS PLAN WITH THE FINDINGS!!!
- [x] **GOLD-ADAPT-CLAW-01** Crash-safe proactive-delivery idempotency — claudeclaw `src/pending-resume.ts` persists a pending wake-up send + **renames the file to `.consumed` BEFORE running**, so a crash mid-delivery never double-fires on restart. *Step:* audit NEOTH's proactive channel-delivery (`G-01` channel-delivery / `SPEC-11` LiveDelivery / `Action::ProactiveChannelSend` egress) for the double-fire window: can a crash between "decided to send" and "sent" re-fire the same proactive message on next daemon start? If yes, close it with a rename-before-consume / WAL-ack-gate guard (NEOTH already has the WAL — a `consumed` marker or an ACK-before-send frame suffices). *Effort:* S-M (an audit + a guard). *test:* a simulated crash between decide+send does not re-deliver on restart. — *origin:* claudeclaw — ✅ **DONE (2026-06-15).** **Verify-first confirmed the gap is REAL:** `run_proactive_delivery_tick` was deliberately at-least-once (queue saved LAST; a crash after send + before save re-drains + RE-SENDS; the doc claimed `dedup_key` "bounds duplicate harm" but that dedup is **enqueue-side only** — it never prevents the cross-restart re-fire). **Design (3-lens gremium → at-most-once via inflight claim files):** before each live send the tick atomically writes `~/.neoth/proactive_inflight/<sha256(dedup_key)>.claimed` (via `util::atomic_write`); the next tick's `evict_inflight_claimed` runs BEFORE `drain()`, finds any surviving claim (= a send the daemon crashed through), evicts that key from the queue **without resending**, and records a `crash_recovered` sidecar line — `is_failure` (critical-alert) items carry `was_failure: true` so they are **operator-visible, never silently lost** (the panel's key concern; matches the CLAW-01 acceptance "does not re-deliver on restart"). **⚠ PROCESS NOTE:** the gremium agent **autonomously wrote the full implementation** instead of only advising (the known advisory-panel-not-read-only Gotcha) — and the PC crashed before it ever compiled. **Verify-first the panel caught a real bug:** the agent deleted each claim PER-ITEM right after its send, but the queue is saved once at batch end → on a multi-item batch (cap=3) a crash after item 1/2 sent but before save left their claims already deleted + queue unsaved → items 1/2 **re-drained and double-fired** (the guarantee only held for the last item). **Fixed:** claims are deleted ONLY after the durable queue save (batch-wide `claimed_keys`), so a crash anytime before the save leaves the WHOLE batch's claims → all evicted, none re-sent. Also cleaned a smell (the eviction's `tempfile` "/dev/null" sidecar-fallback → warn-once-and-skip) + a clippy fix (`io::Error::other`). New regression test `evict_drops_an_entire_multi_item_batch_no_resend` pins the multi-item fix; the async tick's live crash path isn't unit-testable (concrete channel types, no mock seam — same limit as the pre-existing tick), so coverage is the eviction-level + structural-ordering guarantee. *files:* `daemon/proactive_dispatcher.rs` (reuses existing `util::atomic_write` + the `proactive_delivered.jsonl` sidecar; **NO** new WAL event, NO config/mod.rs/channels touch). daemon::proactive_dispatcher **28/0**, clippy `-D warnings` clean. Hot-zone-free — disjoint of the parallel CHANNELS lane.

- [x] **GOLD-ADAPT-GOOSE-01** OSV supply-chain malware gate before npm installs — goose `agents/extension_malware_check.rs` queries `api.osv.dev/v1/query` for MAL-* advisories before any `npx`/`uvx`, blocks on confirmed hits, fails-open on network error. NEOTH MISSING: `installers/mod.rs:171` runs `npm install -g <pkg>` (claude-cli/codex/gemini-cli) with ZERO pre-install check; `security/dangerous_command.rs` is shell-pattern-only, no advisory lookup. *Step:* new `security/osv_check.rs` (~120 LOC) — `deny_if_malicious(name, ecosystem, version)` POST to OSV, paginate, filter MAL-*, fail-open on HTTP error; wire into `installers/mod.rs::install_via_npm` before spawn; gate at `AutonomyLevel >= Standard`. *Effort:* ~2h + wiremock tests. **HIGH value** (supply-chain security; directly fits the operator's security-researcher profile + NEOTH's self-contained wizard installs). *test:* a MAL-flagged package is denied; a clean package + a network failure both proceed. — *origin:* goose — ✅ **DONE** (2026-06-13): new `security/osv_check.rs` — `check_package(name, ecosystem, version) -> OsvVerdict::{Clean,Malicious{advisories},Unknown{reason}}` POSTs to `api.osv.dev/v1/query` (reqwest 0.12, 6 s timeout) + pure `classify_osv_body` that collects `vulns[].id` starting `MAL-` (CVE/GHSA are NOT install-blockers — only curated malware). FAILS OPEN (`Unknown`) on build/request/HTTP-status/parse error so an offline/air-gapped install is never bricked. Wired into `installers/mod.rs::install_via_npm` via the testable `npm_supply_chain_gate(package, verdict)` BEFORE the npm spawn: a confirmed `MAL-*` hit `bail!`s (hard block), `Unknown` warns + proceeds, `Clean` proceeds. **DEVIATION from "gate at AutonomyLevel >= Standard":** the block is UNCONDITIONAL (not autonomy-gated) because NEOTH's npm installs are its OWN fixed toolchain (claude-cli/codex/gemini-cli) — those have no legitimate reason to be malware-flagged at ANY autonomy level, so blocking always is strictly safer than goose's arbitrary-`npx`/`uvx` model (where an autonomy carve-out makes sense). 10 tests: 3 `classify_osv_body` (MAL-only filter / CVE+GHSA→Clean / empty+missing-vulns→Clean) + 4 `check_package_at` via wiremock (MAL→Malicious, no-vulns→Clean, HTTP-500→Unknown, unreachable-host→Unknown) + 3 `npm_supply_chain_gate` (Malicious blocks naming the advisory, Clean allows, Unknown fails-open). Full lib **7739 passed / 0 failed**; clippy clean. New `security/osv_check.rs` + `security/mod.rs` reg + `installers/mod.rs` gate — disjoint of all parallel-hot files.

NEOTH's checks (`repetition_guard.rs`, risk-gate+egress scan in `dispatch_loop.rs:219`, allowlist/autonomy in `gate.rs`, `smart_approve.rs`) are hardwired sequentially — a new check means editing the dispatch loop. *Step:* add `ToolInspector` trait + `ToolInspectorChain` called at the top of the per-call block in `dispatch_loop.rs`; port `ToolRepetitionGuard`→`RepetitionInspector` + the dangerous-command/egress block→`RiskPolicyInspector`; the ~30-line hardwired block becomes `chain.run(call)`. Benefit: new tool-safety checks bolt on without touching the loop + each emits a typed loggable verdict. *test:* existing repetition+risk behaviour preserved through the chain; a new test-inspector's Deny blocks a call. — *origin:* goose — ✅ **DONE (2026-06-15, fresh + security-reviewer pass).** New `mcp/tool_inspection.rs`: `ToolInspector` trait (`Send`) + `ToolInspectorChain` (first-block-wins, insertion order) + `RepetitionInspector` (wraps GOLD-ADOPT-20 guard) + `RiskPolicyInspector` (wraps GOLD-ADOPT-23 `inspect_tool_args`+`evaluate_tool_risk`, surfaces the dangerous/egress warns). `dispatch_loop.rs` per-call block now calls `inspectors.inspect(call, security_policy)`; a new pure safety check bolts on by implementing the trait + joining the chain (test `custom_pure_inspector_bolts_on_without_a_loop_edit` proves it). **SECURITY-CRITICAL design choice (verify-first):** the chain only COMPUTES the verdict; the NEOTH-specific risk-confirm **LEASE lift + the 4 distinct WAL audit emits stay VERBATIM in the loop** (`if let Risk { risk, gate }` arm) — they are async + stateful authorization (filesystem leases, the WAL writer), not a pure inspection, so moving them would have risked the gate. Allowlist/autonomy/SmartApprove stay in `invoke_with_audit` (need the live McpClient) as planned. **Independent security-reviewer pass → VERDICT SHIP**, 0 CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM; all 6 invariants traced + PASS (behavior-preserving · repetition-before-risk order + counter ticks once · warns always fire · NO bypass to `dispatch_one` · lease single-use+fail-closed unchanged · `Send` across `.await`). Applied the reviewer's LOW-1 hardening: a compile-time exhaustiveness `match &inspection` guard so a future verdict variant can NEVER silently fall through to dispatch (converts it to a compile error). *Reviewer LOW-3 (follow-up, PRE-EXISTING — not this refactor):* `consume_risk_leases` dual-lease audit records the dangerous lease id when both were consumed — audit-precision only, no bypass; tracked for a tiny separate fix. *files:* `mcp/tool_inspection.rs` (new), `mcp/dispatch_loop.rs`, `mcp/mod.rs`. `mcp:: 196/0` (5 new inspector tests + the existing gate/lease/WAL tests all green), clippy `-D warnings` clean. `mcp/*`-only — disjoint of the parallel CHANNELS lane.

**From RecursiveMAS** (https://github.com/RecursiveMAS/RecursiveMAS — deep-read 2026-06-19; cloned `CascadeProjects/RecursiveMAS`; **NO LICENSE FILE = all-rights-reserved → cannot vendor/redistribute code or weights**):

- [x] **Alex-RMAS-00** ✅ DEEP-READ DONE (2026-06-19; read `modeling.py`/`inference_mas.py`/`run.py`/`prompts.py`/`system_loader.py`). **What it ACTUALLY is (verify-first, from the code — NOT the marketing):** a **trained-weights latent-space** multi-agent method, *not* a prompt-orchestration library. Role-specialised small models (Planner-Qwen3-1.7B / Critic-Llama3.2-1B / Solver-Qwen2.5-Math-1.5B class) exchange **hidden-state tensors** through two tiny trained `nn.Module` adapters — `Adapter`(`ln_res_adapter`, the per-model "innerlink": pre_ln→proj1→GELU→proj2→residual→post_ln) and `CrossModelAdapter`(`outer_ln_res_adapter`, the cross-model "outerlink": projects model-A hidden_dim→model-B hidden_dim) — injected into prompt slots (`<<LATENT_PLANNER_SLOT>>`/`<<LATENT_REFINED_SLOT>>`/`<<LATENT_FEEDBACK_SLOT>>`) and refined across `num_recursive_rounds` (default 3) × `latent_length` (16-48) latent tokens. 4 patterns: Sequential / Mixture / Deliberation(needs `TAVILY_API_KEY`) / Distillation. Stack: torch 2.9 + transformers 5.3 + HF checkpoint downloads. **Two hard consequences for adoption:** (1) **NOT portable to Claude-Code/text-API agent orchestration** (incl. *this* machine's agent use) — the method needs raw hidden-state access; Claude subagents are text-API, no latent access. Only the *topology* (the 4 patterns) transfers, and NEOTH's council + Workflow already implement those at the prompt level. (2) **For NEOTH it fits ONLY as an optional, resource-gated, opt-in LOCAL backend** — exactly the operator's constraint ("auf neoth NUR wenn lokal genug resourcen da sind und der user es will"). License=null blocks vendoring → must be invoked as an *operator-installed external sidecar*, never bundled. Local env set up on this machine (`CascadeProjects/RecursiveMAS/.venv-rmas`, RTX 3090 24GB present) for benchmarking; it does **not** drive Claude-Code agents. — *origin:* RecursiveMAS

### Deep-read derived adaptations (2026-06-12) — ALL 1.0-GOLD scope

> 6 code-level deep-reads (Alex-00..06 + Jarvis/Veronica/LOWKEY brains). Full evidence (file:line, coverage, license) in `REVIEWS/_gold_audit/_alex_repos_2026-06-12/{openhuman,hermes,jarvis_veronica_lowkey,mnemo,odysseus,rscrypto}.md`. License discipline: odysseus = AGPLv3 → re-implement logic/prompts as fresh Rust, NEVER paste; mnemo = MIT; rscrypto = unaudited private re-impl → do NOT add as dep (steal 3 patterns only); openhuman/hermes/jarvis/veronica/lowkey = operator's own.
> **Highest leverage first:** ADAPT-HERMES-01 (operator crons) · ADAPT-OH-01 (migration-first onboarding) · ADAPT-MEM-01..06 (fact-state-machine, contradiction ledger, parallel recall, DSPM scoring, pre-compact backup, knowledge-graph) · ADAPT-ODY-04/09/06 (stall watchdog, context compactor, incognito) · ADAPT-ODY-05+GOLD-FEAT-08 (refusal→abliterated fallback, TODO:40-43).

#### ▶ Builder's guide — READ THIS before picking any GOLD-ADAPT item

**Per-item workflow:** (1) Open the source evidence `REVIEWS/_gold_audit/_alex_repos_2026-06-12/<repo>.md` — it has the exact source `file:line` + the algorithm/prompt to port. (2) **Verify-first** the NEOTH integration point still exists — `grep`/read the named module+fn before trusting any line number (the 2026-06-12 ARCH-01 refactor moved ~1100 LOC; refs drift; design notes can be wrong). (3) Port the LOGIC, not the source (AGPLv3 odysseus: re-implement in Rust, NEVER paste). (4) Add a `#[cfg(test)]` test proving the *test:* acceptance line. (5) `cargo clippy -p neothd --tests --no-deps -- -D warnings` + `cargo test -p neothd --lib` green via the §1 vcvars recipe (feature-gated code also needs `--features X`). (6) Flip `[ ]`→`[x]` here AND in `PROGRESS_v1_0_GOLD.md` in the SAME commit.

**Recurring integration patterns (the "how" for most items — wire to these, don't reinvent):**
- **New daemon cron** (HERMES-06/07/16, MEM-02/07/11/12/15, OH-07): `daemon/<name>_cron.rs` exposing `pub async fn spawn_<name>_cron(cfg, [provider,] writer) -> Option<JoinHandle<..>>` that returns `None` when its `freedom.yaml` flag is off; add a 1-line `serve_tasks::spawn_<name>(...)` delegate; call it in `run_serve`, register the handle in `worker_watch`, abort it in `serve_tasks::shutdown_background_tasks` (preserve abort ORDER). Mirror an existing cron (e.g. `spawn_monitor_cron`, `spawn_drift_alert_cron`).
- **New config field** (OH-11, G-01, LOWKEY-08): add to the right struct in `config/mod.rs` (or a `config/*.rs` domain file) with field-level `#[serde(default)]` so existing `freedom.yaml` still parses; read at the consumer; never required. Add a default + roundtrip + yaml-parse test.
- **New WAL event** (MEM-02/05, plugin events): **5 sites** (§2) — const + compile-time band-assert + `EVENT_NAME_TABLE` + `all_event_codes_are_unique` + emit-site; pick a free slot from §2 (`0x5D-5F`, `0x69-6F`, …) and coordinate the band FIRST.
- **New CLI subcommand** (`neoth cron`, …): enum variant in `cli/mod.rs` + `mod` decl + dispatch arm (inject `global_output`); model it on `cli/fact_check.rs` (WIRE-11). Adding/removing a command → run `NEOTH_REGEN_CLI_DOCS=1 cargo test -p neothd --lib cli_commands_md_is_up_to_date` (§1).
- **GUI feature** (ODY-04/05/09/10/11, OH-08/12): the Slint **3-file pattern** (see the shipped ODY-01/02/03 above for a worked example) — `neothd-gui/ui/*.slint` (component + struct fields + callbacks), `neothd-gui/src/panel_logic.rs` (a serde-decoupled mirror struct + load fn), `neothd-gui/src/main.rs` (ModelRc builder + worker-thread load + `on_*` handler). Backend feeds the GUI by EXTENDING the `cli/chat.rs --stream` sentinel + updating `strip_stream_sentinel` in `main.rs`. `neothd-gui` is a BIN crate → `cargo test -p neothd-gui` (no `--lib`); set `NEOTH_LOG=error` so daemon stdout-tracing can't corrupt the GUI subprocess JSON parser.
- **New memory module** (MEM-01/03/06/07, OH-10): `memory/<name>.rs` + `pub mod` in `memory/mod.rs`; recall integration in `cli/recall.rs::run_recall`; keep GUI-facing structs serde-decoupled; respect the GDPR `forget` cascade (a new table must be wiped by `memory/forget.rs`).
- **New provider** (ODY-15 copilot): `providers/<name>.rs` implementing the provider trait; register in `providers::from_config`; add a wizard step in `cli/init.rs`; honour cost-free-first + the consent gate (`consent::is_granted`) + the n8n consent gate.

**Gotchas (§2 + 2026-06-12 audit):** `&s[..N]` panics on multibyte UTF-8 → `chars().take(N)`; stream/pastejack guards must be `chars()`-based; a default-OFF Cargo feature ships only if the release step adds `--features X`; never hold a SQLite/Mutex guard across `.await`; new blocking `std::fs`/syscalls on the async reactor need `spawn_blocking`; new cloud egress must pass `consent::is_granted` at EVERY autonomy level (not just Strict); `forget --physical` and `verify` must fail-LOUD on tamper-suspect WAL, never silently.

**Build order / dependencies (do the unlockers first):**
1. **HERMES-01 (operator crons)** is the keystone — once it lands, HERMES-06/07/16 + MEM-16 become *operator-cron prompts* (effort S, not L).
2. MEM-06 (knowledge graph) before MEM-14; MEM-01 (fact-state-machine) before MEM-05 + LOWKEY-05 groundtruth checks; MEM-04 (5-LOC scoring) is the easiest first win.
3. ODY-05 (metrics chip) consumes HERMES-09 (metering); OH-01 (migration) pairs with GOLD-FEAT-04.
4. **Refusal-fallback bundle** (the TODO:40-43 pain): ODY-08 teacher-escalation + LOWKEY-06 PME + G-03 abliterated model → ship together under GOLD-FEAT-08.

**License per source:** odysseus = AGPLv3 (re-implement, never paste) · mnemo = MIT (port freely) · rscrypto = NO dep (3 patterns as local code only) · openhuman/hermes/jarvis/veronica/lowkey = operator's own.

##### ⚠ RECONCILIATION & COLLISION MAP — READ BEFORE implementing any MEM/JV-MEM item (operator concern: don't let adaptations clash)

- **UNIFIED GRAPH (MEM-06 vs JV-MEM-08):** ONE table `idx_memory_graph(from,to,type:entity_relation|hebbian_coAccess,weight,count,...)`. **MEM-06** (mnemo LLM-extract) writes `entity_relation` at ingest; **JV-MEM-08** (Hebbian) writes `hebbian_coAccess` at recall; BFS traverses both. **Order: MEM-06 schema FIRST, JV-MEM-08 adds the hebbian write-path.** Never build two graphs.

- [x] **GOLD-ADAPT-MEM-07b** (NEW, sub-step of MEM-07) — mining-time co-occurrence edge bootstrap: walk `idx_episode` in time-windows, write initial edge weights `= 0.15 × co_occurrence_count` so the association graph has edges BEFORE live recall accrues (fresh-install practicality). *src:* `mempalace/hallways.py:161-321`. S. NEW. — ✅ **DONE** (2026-06-13, research-panel-grounded: 2-agent algorithm+pitfalls). `assoc_graph::bootstrap_co_occurrence(conn, window_ns, now_unix)`: streaming scan of `idx_episode ORDER BY ts_ns ASC`, tumbling 1 h buckets (`DEFAULT_BOOTSTRAP_WINDOW_NS=3_600_000_000_000`), accumulate canonical `(lo<hi)` pairs per bucket into a `HashMap`, then `INSERT … ON CONFLICT(lo_id,hi_id) DO NOTHING` each pair (weight `min(0.15×count, 0.9)`). **The `DO NOTHING` is the load-bearing coexistence guarantee** — bootstrap NEVER touches a live-learned or prior-bootstrapped edge, so it's idempotent on re-run + can't inflate a live weight. Dense windows (>20 episodes = ingestion burst) skipped whole; global 50 000-pair backstop. **Verify-first correction the research missed:** each episode has ONE `ts_ns` → one bucket, so a given episode-PAIR co-occurs in ≤1 window → `count≡1` → bootstrap weight is always 0.15 for episode endpoints (the count/clamp machinery is harmless but never >0.15; documented). A 0.15 edge decays below the 0.05 floor in ~4.6 days unless a real co-recall confirms it. Consumer = new one-shot `neoth recall --bootstrap-assoc` short-circuit (mirrors `--assoc`; serve.rs is parallel-hot so no startup hook in this lane). 7 tests (base-weight, idempotent, never-overwrites-live, canonical-normalise, single-episode-noop, no-cross-window-link, dense-window-skip). Full lib **7785 passed / 0 failed**; clippy clean; `memory/assoc_graph.rs` + `cli/recall.rs` only — disjoint of parallel-hot.

Build the prereq slice first (one deliberate item), then the 3 are trivial. **★★ SESSION-ID-IN-WAL-HEADER blueprint (4-lens panel 2026-06-14, the SECOND complementary unblock — frame-grouping, for per-conversation session-health + any WAL-frame-grouped view):** verify-first found `EventHeaderV2.session_id` (bytes 53..69, wire-pinned by the PROG-19 offset test) ALREADY EXISTS but is UNIVERSALLY ZEROED in production — `HeaderBuilder.session()` exists, NO call site sets it. STEPS: add `SessionId::from_str_hash(&str)` (xxh3_64 → first 8 of 16 bytes) in `wal/types.rs`; thread the live `current_session_id` (= `chat-{ts}-{xxh3}`, already in scope at `run_post_reply_pipelines`) via `.session()` into the 0x21 emits at `chat.rs:1450` + `:2464` (both currently `make_header` → switch to the builder), `0x1A` at `refusal_recovery.rs:304` (add `session_id: Option<&str>` param through `try_recover_multi`/`emit_persistent_audit`), `0x27` at `refusal_abliterated.rs:130` (add `Option<SessionId>` param); LEAVE `0x42` cron ZERO (no chat session — correct). Then `session_health_cron::scan_health_by_day` keys `(SessionId, day)` (ZERO = the day-aggregate bucket ONLY, never a per-conversation key) + a `per_session_evaluate` path = the primitive VIEW-01/03/06 + per-conversation health consume. **NO wire-format change (field already allocated), NO new event, NO schema bump, clean historical-ZERO migration (old frames fall back to the day window).** **DEFERRED until VIEW-01/03/06 are actually scheduled (primitive-ahead-of-consumer otherwise) — but FULLY DESIGNED + ship-ready.** This WAL-header `session_id` (frame grouping) is COMPLEMENTARY to the `UsageEvent.session_id` extension above (cost aggregation) — different stores, build the one the consuming view needs.

**LMCache/LMCache** (Apache-2.0 Python+CUDA; steal ALGORITHM not code — NEOTH is Rust+candle, single-operator). Hard-skips: CacheBlend (per-layer interception incompatible w/ candle-eager + Ouro recurrent loop), P2P/disaggregated (no single-op consumer), `raw_block` Rust (Linux io_uring+PyO3), CUDA kernels.

This is the honest reconciliation, not a wholesale revert.

**✅ RESIDUAL RE-VERIFICATION (2026-06-20, operator "mach alle residuals"):** the 45-row residual triage queue (`wsv_triage_{queue,results}.json`) split into **16 "apply" (REAL fix) + 29 "plan-close" (non-actionable)**. Each REAL fix was re-verified against CURRENT code (HEAD `a7c4c02`, ~2 weeks past the `35c94d2` triage base) — and **14 of the 16 were ALREADY RESOLVED in the interim** (the team kept shipping). Applying their stale fix-designs would have been redundant or HARMFUL (re-adding present code / the designs themselves cited fabricated APIs like `gate.check_sync`). Verified-fixed-since-base: **GR-082** (probe_cmd already owns the Child + kill+wait path, comment cites GR-082) · **GR-021** (dispatcher.rs:506 now *handles* the `apply_outcome` error, not `?`-propagates) · **GR-122** (`name_session_best_effort` now clears the `PaidProviderCall` gate) · **GR-052** (pm-shipping-artifacts slash-cmd refs removed) · **GR-068** (hyperswarm EOF now typed `io.kind()==UnexpectedEof`, not Display-string) · **GR-041** (credentials.rs has all 4 `inference_*_key` fields + merge) · **GR-010** (`InboundDedup` struct + dedup live in webhook_listener) · **GR-049** (registry watches freedom.yaml NonRecursive, comment cites GR-049) · **GR-164** (`replay_once_audited` + `EVENT_TYPE_INDEXER_TAMPER_SUSPECT`) · **GR-110** (pm-pre-mortem trigger now "preparing for **product** launch") · **GR-140** (GOLD-FEAT-01 already `[x]` + superseding WAL note) · **DD-05** (troubleshooting.md has no broken `--features cli,wal,channels` line) · **DD-12** (plugins.md has the wasm-plugin-host callout) · **DD-06** (dashboard already recomputed — WS-F now 20/8/12, not the stale 16/16). **GENUINE REMAINING GAPS (2):** (a) **GR-069** — `coding/provider_worker.rs` `provider.complete()` at :93 + :146 carry NO autonomy gate / WAL audit (ungated paid calls in the coding worker). Scoped fix: add `wal_writer: WalWriterRef` + `autonomy: Option<AutonomyLevel>` fields + `with_*` builders (None-default → no test breakage) to `ProviderWorker`, gate each call via `Gate::for_level(level).with_confirm(auto_confirm()).check(&Action::PaidProviderCall{eur_estimate:0.0}, Some(writer)).await` (the gate emits its own audit WAL — mirror GR-122); thread `cfg.autonomy` + writer at the `code.rs:405` construction site (verify a WAL writer is in scope there — if not, autonomy-gate-only). (b) **GR-012** — webhook inbound has no pre-dispatch durable spool, so a crash between accept + dispatch loses the message; needs a `~/.neoth/inbound_spool/<wamid>.json` write before the dispatch future + a startup drain. Both are bounded multi-file security/durability changes left for a focused (non-context-degraded) pass. The 29 "plan-close" rows are INTENTIONAL(12)/ALREADY_FIXED(5)/OVERSTATED(11)/STALE(1) — non-actionable, recorded in the triage file.

### HIGH (16) — confirmed; security 🔒 first
- [x] **GOLD-SEC-08** 🔒 ✅ FIXED — channels::rate_limit bucket map now bounded (MAX_BUCKETS=10_000 + evict_if_needed wired into try_consume; idle-TTL sweep + live_bucket_count accessor + regression test); the A-18 unique-sender flood DoS is closed on the live inbound path. bd068f8.
- [x] **GR-003** 🔒 ✅ FIXED — n8n provider_call cloud-check now routes through the canonical EXHAUSTIVE `consent::is_cloud` (was an inline matches! omitting AnthropicApi+Cohere → those skipped the Strict consent gate). is_some_and(consent::is_cloud); compile-enforced no-drift. n8n_api::handlers 5/0; clippy clean.
- [x] **GR-005** 🔒 ✅ FIXED — `forget` now rebuilds the on-disk HNSW snapshot from the wiped SQLite (new `embeddings::rebuild_snapshot_if_present(conn, home)`, called in run_forget when embedding_rows>0), so forgotten vectors are purged from the searchable index, not just idx_embedding. Best-effort (SQLite already wiped; cold-load falls back). Tests: rebuild_snapshot_if_present_purges_forgotten_vectors + noop-without-snapshot. clippy clean.
- [x] **GR-007** 🔒 ✅ FIXED — verify is no longer fail-open on a corrupt segment header. verify_segments only skipped <SEGMENT_HEADER_LEN stubs; a ≥60-byte segment whose header didn't parse fell through logical_segment_bytes' silent raw@0 fallback → 0 markers found → reported clean (a corrupted-header, esp. compressed, segment evaded detection). Now an unparseable header on a real-sized segment is recorded as a tamper-suspect FAILURE (run_verify then bails). logical_segment_bytes untouched (indexer's legit bare-stream handling preserved). Test verify_flags_a_corrupt_header_segment_as_tamper_suspect; cli::verify 17/0; clippy clean.
- [x] **GR-008** 🔒 ✅ FIXED — forget --physical no longer reports success when a segment refused redaction. The output ignored the errors field (incremented when a sealed/compressed segment refuses redaction or an audit-marker emit fails) and printed the affirmative 'no matching WAL frames — SQLite-only forget was sufficient' / 'GDPR-grade erasure' regardless. Now: errors count printed; an INCOMPLETE warning replaces the success message when errors>0; and the command FAILS LOUD (non-zero exit via bail) so an operator/script never mistakes a partial/refused redaction for a confirmed wipe (pure physical_erasure_incomplete predicate drives both). Test physical_erasure_incomplete_when_any_error; cli::memory 5/0; clippy clean.
- [x] **GR-018** 🔒 ✅ FIXED — SmartApprove confirm-bypass is now per-server: global security.smart_approve is the MASTER switch (allocates the cache), and the bypass fires only when the specific McpServerConfig.smart_approve is ALSO true (gate.rs:277 `cfg.smart_approve && smart_approve_is_readonly`). Enabling it on one trusted server no longer bypasses confirm for the rest. New per-server field (serde default false, 10 literals) + master-switch doc + regression test smart_approve_is_per_server_opt_in. mcp:: 185/0; clippy -D warnings clean.
- [x] **GR-027** 🔒 ✅ FIXED — from_config_for_utility no longer leaks the main provider key to a different utility vendor. Refactored into a pure build_utility_config that strips provider_key/endpoint/model when the utility kind differs from the main kind (require_provider_key then resolves the utility's OWN key or fails cleanly → best-effort caller falls back); same-vendor flagship→fast keeps the shared key. Tests utility_config_strips_main_key_for_a_different_vendor + keeps_key_for_same_vendor; providers:: 587/0; clippy clean.
- [x] **GR-032** 🔒 ✅ FIXED — risk-override leases are now bounded + single-use: (1) dangerous_command/egress grants are hard-capped at MAX_RISK_OVERRIDE_TTL_SECS=24h via LeaseScope::check_ttl, enforced in both 'neoth lease grant' and 'neoth risk-confirm' (--ttl 9999d rejected); (2) a lease that lifts a block is CONSUMED on use (consume_risk_leases revokes+persists in dispatch_loop), so the window authorises exactly ONE blocked call, not unlimited until expiry. Per-command scope is N/A to a pre-auth window — single-use bounds the blast radius. Tests risk_override_scopes_are_ttl_capped + over_cap_ttl_is_rejected + single-use assert; lease 10/0, risk_confirm 6/0, dispatch_loop 17/0; clippy clean.
- [x] **GR-033** 🔒 ✅ FIXED — neoth permissions audit no longer swallows the WAL-scan tamper-suspect error. for_each_frame's Result was 'let _ ='-discarded; it Errs only on an unreconstructable (tamper-suspect) compressed blob, so the audit silently reported a clean trail over a corrupt segment (fail-open). The Result is now propagated with context (audit_segment returns Err → the command fails loud); benign paths unaffected (for_each_frame still breaks cleanly on torn tails). Test audit_segment_fails_loud_on_a_tamper_suspect_compressed_segment; permissions::audit 15/0; clippy clean.
- [x] **GR-043** 🔒 ✅ FIXED — staged-apply no longer bypasses the SEC-10 signature gate: (1) the require-signature + no-pinned-key fail-closed bail now runs BEFORE both staged + fresh paths (was fresh-only, which the staged fast-path returned before reaching); (2) apply_from_staged RE-VERIFIES the minisign signature at apply time vs the in-binary pinned key (stage_update now persists the .minisig; the attacker-writable signature_status in pending.json is no longer trusted) → a planted/swapped staged archive can't be applied. Tests: apply_from_staged_refuses_when_signature_required_but_unverifiable (+no-swap) + tamper + legacy-parse; self_update 61/0, cli::update 12/0; clippy clean.
- [x] **GR-055** 🔒 ✅ FIXED — a deeplink-sourced (untrusted) recipe that injects a 'system' instructions override now requires an explicit operator PREVIEW + confirm before the agentic run (confirm_untrusted_deeplink gate in run_one, fired by the pure deeplink_system_override_needs_confirm predicate); fail-closed when stdin isn't a TTY (refuses rather than running untrusted system instructions unattended). Deeplinks without a system override + local-file recipes are unaffected. Tests deeplink_with_system_override_needs_confirm + confirm_untrusted_deeplink_fails_closed_when_not_a_tty; cli::recipe 7/0; clippy clean.
- [x] **GR-058** 🔒 ✅ FIXED — redact no longer fails-open on a corrupt segment header. The Err arm of the header-len derivation fell back to SEGMENT_HEADER_LEN (60), so a corrupt v2 header (61) misaligned the first frame → MAGIC failed → the loop broke → SILENT null-redaction while memory::forget reported success (privacy hole). Now a segment ≥ SEGMENT_HEADER_LEN whose header doesn't parse is REFUSED loudly (like the sealed-compressed case) → forget surfaces the error (GR-008); a sub-header file keeps the benign no-op. Test scan_refuses_a_segment_with_an_unparseable_header; wal::redact 12/0; clippy clean.
- [x] **GR-002** ✅ FIXED — the auto-promote gate (COR-03) can no longer be bypassed by an empty patch + self-reported green tests. The apply path stamped applied=cfg.test_cmd.is_some() even for an empty patch (which apply_patch_via_worktree returns Ok for WITHOUT running any worktree suite) → a no-op auto-promoted to DONE. Now applied requires BOTH a configured test_cmd AND a non-empty patch (pure apply_is_test_verified); an empty patch stays applied=false → check_auto_promotable blocks it (NotApplied). Test empty_patch_is_never_test_verified; coding::dispatcher 35/0; clippy clean.
- [x] **GR-006** ✅ FIXED — a stale pre-ARCH-03 wal_cursor below the real header_len (e.g. 60 vs a 61-byte v2 header) no longer perpetually stalls the indexer mid-header. The resume offset is clamped up to header_len (start_offset.max(header_len)), so a stale/short cursor self-heals to the first real frame; a normal resume cursor is unchanged. Test replay_self_heals_a_stale_cursor_below_header_len (plants cursor=60 on a 61-byte-header v2 segment → indexes both frames); memory::indexer 6/0; clippy clean.
- [x] **DD-01** ✅ FIXED — import-format.md now states the real command surface up front (status banner: only 'neoth-migrate dry-run --manifest' works today; 'apply' is preview-only/refuses with the real WAL-writing import post-v1.0; 'export' not yet implemented) + the runnable example and §5 export section are corrected/caveated — no longer presents the import/export pipeline as shipped.
- [x] **DD-02** ✅ FIXED — quickstart/install/getting-started.md no longer instruct a bare 'cargo install neoth' (which README:54 denies is published); all three now show the bootstrap installer / from-source 'cargo install --path neothd' with the not-yet-on-crates.io caveat, consistent with README.

- [x] **GR-128** (medium) ✅ FIXED — `LoopOutcome.hit_cap` stayed `false` when a grind run was cut at `max_iterations` via the clean-exit branch (the model emits no tool calls and the nudge is gated on `iterations < max_iterations`, so it broke at the `extraction.is_empty()` path with `hit_cap` unset). - [x] **GR-012** (medium) ✅ FIXED (comment) — `DISPATCH_GATE.acquire()` runs INSIDE the spawned task, so the gate bounds concurrent EXECUTION, not the spawn COUNT — the comment's "restores the no-unbounded-tokio::spawn invariant" claim was false. verify-first KILLED the proposed acquire-before-spawn fix: the 200 ACK is already returned regardless, so dropping an over-cap webhook would silently LOSE it (Meta doesn't redeliver an ACKed message) — queue-on-gate is the deliberate lesser evil. Fixed the comment to state this accurately; no behaviour change. `channels/webhook_listener.rs`.
- [x] **GR-011** (medium) ✅ NUANCED — the ACK-before-persistence crash window (a hard crash between Meta's 200 and the first in-task WAL write loses the message, no retry) is REAL but the ACK-first pattern is INTENTIONAL (GOLD-COR-08, prevents Meta retry storms → duplicate pipeline runs). The agent's minimal fix (a pre-spawn receipt frame) reuses `0x32 CHANNEL_INGRESS` — which the memory indexer parses per-message, so a batch-receipt risks mis-parse; the channels band 0x30-3F is FULL (a distinct event = 5-site work) and the fix is auditability-only (no replay/recovery). A durable inbound spool + restart replay is the real closure and a follow-on feature, not a blind fix. Documented; no code. `channels/webhook_listener.rs`.
